a:T485,The technical contribution is a clean training framework rather than a new architecture. The paper keeps architecture, model size, tokenizer, and overall language mixture fixed, while varying two main factors: the L1 identity and the order in which the two languages are presented during pretraining. English is always the L2, and the suite spans 21 L1s. The authors train under five exposure regimes, covering more interleaved versus more staged curricula, at three FineWeb scales: 100M, 2B, and 24B tokens, plus a human-scale 100M condition. A strong design choice is the inclusion of matched monolingual baselines for 20 languages, which helps separate genuine bilingual transfer from simple data-composition effects. Conceptually, the framework unifies structured exposure and sequential bilingual training ideas from earlier work, but treats exposure timing itself as the primary intervention. Evaluation is also broader than perplexity: the main target is psycholinguistic alignment, especially L2 eye-tracking reading times on MECO, with additional probes on cross-lingual structural priming and CEFR-stratified learner-error discrimination on BLiSS.b:T4d9,The most consistent result is that ordered or staged exposure tends to align better with human L2 reading times than fully interleaved training. The paper argues this effect is not explained away by higher overall proficiency or better language balance, which is important because those are the obvious confounds. In other words, the point in training when a language is introduced appears to be a meaningful determinant of human-like L2 processing behavior. A second important finding is that the curriculum effect does not transfer uniformly across tasks. On structural priming and BLiSS learner-error discrimination, balanced exposure is often competitive or even better. So staged curricula seem to shape specific processing dynamics rather than universally improving bilingual competence. In scale terms, the suite includes 285 bilingual and 45 monolingual models across 100M, 2B, and 24B tokens, all with checkpoint trajectories. The paper also compares against larger massively multilingual models and reports that carefully designed bilingual curricula can match or exceed their L2 reading-time alignment under much smaller parameter budgets. For researchers working on scaling and data curricula, that is the most actionable result.c:T424,Read this as an experimental-design paper. Start with the setup sections and verify exactly what is held fixed and what is varied; that is where the credibility of the later claims comes from. Then focus on the exposure-regime definitions, data-scale settings, construction of matched monolingual baselines, and checkpoint release strategy. In the experiments, prioritize the MECO reading-time analysis, since that is the main empirical claim. Pay attention to how the authors rule out alternative explanations such as proficiency and balance. After that, read the structural priming and BLiSS sections to understand where the curriculum effect does and does not generalize. The most useful deeper questions are: whether exposure schedule should be treated as an independent scaling axis, whether early checkpoints reveal transfer-emergence phases, and whether matched monolingual controls can be imported into broader multilingual or continual-pretraining studies. A skim takes about 25–35 minutes; the figures and appendix are where much of the value sits.d:T49d,The reported results are organized around four operational questions. First, CPT does acquire new knowledge. The effect is strongest for OLMo2 and Gemma-3 1B, while the Llama-family gains are smaller. On pre-cutoff questions, five of six models also improve, which argues against broad factual forgetting. Gains are larger for more recent events and persist even when the source articles are not in the training corpus, reducing the likelihood that the result is only direct memorization. Second, the cost to general capability is small: macro-averaged accuracy over 13 downstream tasks stays within 0.01 of the base model for every model, and 11 of the 13 tasks remain within ±0.02. Third, the training recipe shows clear tradeoffs. A curated 6B-token slice captures most of the available signal; one learning rate cannot jointly optimize new-knowledge acquisition and general benchmark retention; sufficiently high-rank LoRA matches full CPT with much lower memory; and performance plateaus within roughly the first third of training. Fourth, CPT gains survive SFT, but DPO is model-family dependent: it partially erodes gains for Llama-3.1 8B while improving them for OLMo2 7B.e:T781,The paper first analyzes token-level refinement dynamics in a looped Transformer. The architecture has untied prelude and coda stacks, with a middle block of k layers reused for d recurrences, so parameter depth is p+k+l while realized compute depth is p+kd+l. The main experiments use a Huginn-style configuration such as (2, 4⊗32, 2).

To characterize instability, the authors define trace entropy, which measures uncertainty along the loop-wise reasoning trajectory for each token. Incorrect reasoning traces tend to have higher trace entropy. Using a logit-lens analysis, they then identify two token behaviors. Easy tokens improve monotonically across iterations, with growing top-1 vs top-2 margins. Hard tokens do not: they often become confident too early and then attempt revision later, or show unstable refinement patterns. These hard tokens account for only about 10%–15% of the reasoning trace but appear to be disproportionately important.

LoopCD builds on this observation. Standard contrastive decoding uses logits from an expert model and an amateur model. LoopCD replaces that external contrast with an internal one: logits from the final loop iteration serve as the refined distribution, while logits from earlier iterations provide the contrastive signal. The intuition is that later iterations are usually more refined, but earlier iterations may preserve useful intermediate evidence before unstable late-stage refinement distorts it. The final sampling distribution is formed by contrasting these loop-wise logits.

A practical advantage is that no extra model is needed and almost no extra forward cost is incurred, because the intermediate hidden states and logits are already produced during looping. The paper also argues that the gains are concentrated on hard tokens rather than spread uniformly across the sequence, which makes the method more targeted than generic decoding heuristics.f:T5e4,The paper evaluates LoopCD on arithmetic reasoning, code generation, and question answering, and reports consistent gains over standard decoding and stronger decoding baselines. The excerpt provided does not include the full main-table numbers, but several experimental findings are central.

First, trace entropy is strongly associated with reasoning quality. On GSM8K, incorrect reasoning traces show systematically higher entropy than correct ones, suggesting that loop instability leaves a measurable uncertainty signature.

Second, hard tokens are rare but important. They make up roughly 10%–15% of the reasoning trace, yet random replacement experiments in Table 1 show that perturbing hard tokens hurts accuracy much more than perturbing easy tokens. This is one of the strongest pieces of evidence in the paper because it moves beyond correlation and shows that these tokens are closer to the actual bottleneck.

Third, the gains from LoopCD are concentrated on hard tokens. The selective-intervention analysis in Section 5.4 indicates that applying the method to those tokens captures most of the benefit, while intervening on easy tokens yields much smaller returns. That supports the paper’s central mechanism.

Fourth, the method is computationally light. Unlike conventional contrastive decoding, it does not require an auxiliary amateur model. It reuses loop-wise intermediate states already computed by the LoopLM, which is attractive in test-time-compute-heavy latent reasoning settings.10:T446,Start with the Introduction and Motivation. Figure 2 is the backbone of the paper: panel (a) links trace entropy to correct vs incorrect trajectories, panel (b) visualizes easy vs hard token refinement patterns, and panel (c) connects hard tokens to high-entropy behavior. Those figures make the problem setup and the method intuition clear.

Then read the Method section to understand exactly which loop logits are contrasted and how the final distribution is formed. The key question is not the algebra itself, but why earlier iterations can be useful even when the final iteration is nominally more refined. In the experiments, prioritize Table 1 and the selective-intervention analysis; they are the most informative pieces for judging whether the hard-token hypothesis is real.

A useful extension to think about while reading: if earlier loops contain complementary signal, should training explicitly encourage monotonic refinement, or should the model learn token-wise adaptive stopping instead of using a fixed maximum recurrence depth? A focused skim should take about 20–30 minutes.11:T4ba,TreeSpark builds on DSpark. A small bidirectional backbone runs once over a block of masked positions and emits hidden states and base logits U_d for each draft depth. DSpark also has a low-rank Markov head B(x,v)=<W1[x],W2[v]> that adds a transition bias from parent token x to candidate token v. TreeSpark uses this existing Markov head to construct parent-conditioned child distributions for every concrete tree node. Evaluating another parent requires only an embedding lookup and a rank-r matrix-vector product, not another backbone pass. Raw proposal probability q0 is then calibrated with target-verification labels using a two-parameter map p_hat = sigmoid(a logit(q0)+b), fitted only on edges whose ancestors were accepted. Nodes are prioritized by the product of calibrated edge probabilities along the path, and expansion stops when the best remaining path survival score falls below a threshold theta. The runtime selects theta from a load-dependent ladder. For nonzero-temperature decoding, TreeSpark samples siblings without replacement from the residual q_T and reconstructs the proposal during recursive rejection sampling. This avoids the bias introduced by deterministic top-k tree expansion.12:T57b,Experiments use Claude Code with Claude Sonnet and Codex with GPT-5 on real repositories such as textual, jinja2, werkzeug, rich, httpx, and tox. To isolate content compression, the authors hold tool filtering fixed and change the compression floor from min_tokens=512 to a no-op-like 49999. To isolate tool filtering, they toggle it while holding the content path fixed, using strict MCP configuration to reduce tool-block jitter. The local Ollama backend and GPU server produce identical compression outputs; the reported segment ratio is about 0.44 for line-numbered reads, with some content compressed to roughly 26% of its original size. The main empirical result is that tool filtering is the dominant and reliably positive lever, saving about 21K tokens per turn. Content compression is often only a roughly 2% marginal effect in real sessions and is easily drowned by turn-count variance. End-to-end savings are about 25% on a single turn and about 39% by turn five in a long session. The measured scaling is linear in N for tool filtering and approximately quadratic for content compression, with a fitted content saving near 3,350 N^2 tokens and a crossover around N≈6, subject to context-window limits. The recall trap is the main negative term: each exact recall resends the original and wastes one previously sent compressed segment, a fixed cost bounded by the compressed segment length.13:T7fb,方法核心是 Semantic-Geometric Decoupled Routing。作者先从 RoPE attention 的分解出发，把 block affinity 看成两部分：一部分是与内容相关的在线语义证据，另一部分是与相对位置相关的几何偏置。已有方法把两者混在 post-RoPE token 表示里一起池化，SGDR 则显式拆开。

第一步，语义聚合移到 pre-RoPE 空间。也就是先在未旋转的 Q/K 表示上做 block 级汇聚，得到更稳定的 block semantic summary。这样做的原因很直接：pre-RoPE 表示没有相位旋转，均值或其他简单聚合不会破坏高频分量，也不会把相邻 token 的局部顺序信息在池化时互相抵消。

第二步，几何信息不再依赖 pooled post-RoPE 向量隐式携带，而是通过离线 structural prior 重建。论文里给出的做法是根据相对 block 几何关系，预先构造一个相对位置偏置项，在推理时与在线语义分数相加，形成 closed-form routing score。这个设计的关键不是“加一个 bias”，而是把位置建模从在线 token 计算里拿掉，变成离线可缓存的结构先验，因此 routing 成本只和 block 数相关，不需要额外 token-level matching。

第三步，路由完全在 block 级进行。对每个 query block，依据 decoupled score 做 Top-p 或预算约束下的 block 选择，生成 causal block mask，再交给 Triton/block-masked attention kernel 执行。作者强调 routing function 的复杂度必须远低于 O(L^2)，否则 sparse attention 在实际 GPU 上未必比高优化的 dense FlashAttention 更快。

从区别上看，SGDR 和已有方法的分界很清楚。它不是像一些方法那样先粗筛 block 再做 token search，也不是在 post-RoPE pooled score 上做温度缩放或启发式修正，而是直接改写了 block score 的构造方式。理论上，它把“为什么 post-RoPE pooling 会坏掉”解释成高频相位干涉；工程上，它把补救从在线搜索换成离线先验，因此更适合长序列 prefill。14:T753,The method is Semantic-Geometric Decoupled Routing (SGDR). The central idea is to decompose block affinity into two parts: online semantic evidence and offline geometric bias. Existing methods implicitly mix both inside pooled post-RoPE features. SGDR separates them explicitly.

First, semantic aggregation is moved to pre-RoPE space. Query and key features are summarized at the block level before rotary rotation is applied. This avoids destructive interference from averaging rotated vectors, especially in high-frequency dimensions. The result is a more stable semantic summary for each block.

Second, positional structure is reconstructed separately rather than being expected to survive pooling. SGDR introduces an offline structural prior based on relative block geometry. At inference time, this prior is combined with the online semantic score to form a closed-form routing score. The important design choice here is that positional bias is no longer recovered through token-level computation or heuristic correction; it is encoded as a reusable block-level prior.

Third, routing remains fully block-level. For each query block, the router selects top blocks under a sparsity budget and produces a causal block mask, which is then consumed by custom Triton/block-masked attention kernels. This keeps routing cost well below quadratic complexity and avoids the common failure mode where sparse attention loses its advantage because routing itself becomes expensive.

Relative to prior work, SGDR is not a coarse-to-fine method with token refinement, and it is not a calibration trick on top of post-RoPE pooled scores. It changes the score construction itself. The theoretical claim is that post-RoPE pooling destroys useful information through phase cancellation; the engineering claim is that offline geometric priors are a better replacement than online search.15:T5c8,The evaluation covers long-context retrieval, understanding, language modeling, and video reasoning, with context lengths from 4K to 128K. Two results matter most.

First, SGDR stays close to exact full attention in accuracy across the tested range, and is better than or competitive with sparse baselines that depend on heuristic search or post-hoc compensation. The abstract does not list every task-level number, but the paper’s main empirical message is that fully block-level routing does not have to incur a large quality drop if the routing score is constructed correctly.

Second, the routing overhead is very small and remains stable as sequence length grows. The key number reported is that routing time stays below 3.4 ms across all evaluated lengths. At 128K, SGDR achieves a 5.03× speedup over FlashAttention. This is the result to pay attention to, because many sparse methods look attractive in asymptotic complexity but lose in wall-clock time once routing/search overhead is included.

The most informative ablations are likely the following: pre-RoPE vs post-RoPE pooling, to validate the phase-cancellation hypothesis; removing the geometric prior, to show that semantic pooling alone is insufficient; and varying block size or sparsity budget, to expose the quality/latency trade-off. If SGDR remains strong at low attention density, that would support the claim that the decoupled score improves ranking quality rather than merely relying on a denser mask.16:T491,SPS is a training-free latent steering framework. It does not update model weights. Instead, it builds a state-conditioned direction bank offline and injects retrieved steering directions during generation. The offline stage samples reasoning trajectories on a small calibration set and identifies high-uncertainty transitions, which are treated as likely branching points. At those states, the same model proposes multiple continuations using a higher proposal temperature. An LLM judge labels continuations as progress-making or non-progress-making. SPS then contrasts the hidden representations of these two groups to derive steering directions and stores them by prefix-state regions. During online inference, the method monitors the current reasoning prefix, retrieves a suitable direction from the bank, and applies it only at high-uncertainty transitions and at selected layers. Compared with temperature schedules, diverse prompting, or generic latent perturbation, SPS aims to steer exploration toward continuations that make measurable progress. Compared with training-time diversity methods, it avoids extra optimization, special losses, or RL reward design.17:T43b,The central result is that efficiency methods often reduce measured jailbreak harmfulness, but the reduction is frequently superficial. The compressed model may still try to answer the harmful request; it simply loses enough reasoning or generation fidelity that the final answer is incomplete, incoherent, or less actionable. Quantization, pruning, and KV cache compression do not behave identically. More aggressive pruning or compression tends to create stronger capability degradation, while quantization methods differ depending on how well they preserve activation-sensitive weights and outlier behavior. Combining methods is closer to deployment practice, but it also makes interpretation harder because memory reduction, reasoning degradation, and harmfulness reduction can occur together. The evaluation scale is clearly specified: 3 reasoning models, 2 jailbreak benchmarks, 50 prompts per benchmark, 3 decoding temperatures, and a six-level harmfulness rating. The provided text does not include full numeric tables for every method, so exact deltas are not inferred here.18:T518,EvalMem abstracts heterogeneous memory systems into an encoding–retrieval–generation pipeline. The tested system first runs natively: it processes dialogue history, updates its memory store, retrieves context, and generates an answer. EvalMem then diagnoses the completed run using benchmark annotations. The Encoding Examiner checks whether the target fact is present in the memory store. The Retrieval Examiner checks whether the system’s native retriever returns usable evidence. The Generation Examiner tests whether the model can answer when given oracle evidence. An Attribution Agent merges these decisions into an 11-code multi-label defect set, allowing coupled failures while avoiding retrieval blame when the source fact is absent from the store. The framework also separates POS queries, where the system should remember and retrieve a fact, from NEG queries, where the correct behavior is refusal due to insufficient evidence. To make store-level diagnosis more reliable, the Encoding Examiner uses a recall-first agentic RAG routine that searches the original memory store with both the query and the source evidence. The paper also introduces DynaMem-Bench for on-policy dynamic evaluation and MemWiki, a search-friendly auxiliary structure built from a system’s own memory export.19:T43e,The method is simple, and that is part of its strength. The task is formulated as three-way step classification: pos for correct and useful, neu for valid but redundant, and neg for logically or computationally incorrect. For each supervised step, the model takes the hidden state at the step-end token and feeds it into a linear classification head. The only substantive change between the two variants is the self-attention mask. In the causal version, a step representation can only depend on the problem, previous steps, and the current step. In the bidirectional version, it can also use later steps in the same completed solution. Training uses cross-entropy over supervised step-end positions. To isolate the effect of attention direction, the authors run 54 controlled comparisons at 1B to 3B scale, keeping architecture, data, and optimization fixed while changing only the mask. They then scale the same design to LLaDA-8B-Base, yielding LLaDA-PRM. The key idea is not a sophisticated verifier head, but letting each step representation encode downstream consistency evidence.1a:T493,The benchmark defines three knowledge-work personas: Morgan, a startup CEO; Alex, an infrastructure engineer; and Riley, a product manager. Each persona has a multi-year simulated history with 3,400 to 5,128 user messages, around 500k user-message tokens. Evaluation is action-based rather than QA-based. For example, if a user asks the agent to order a small latte without naming a cafe, the agent has to recover an earlier preference and place the order at the right shop. Tools and apps such as Notion, Gmail, GitHub, calendars, CRM systems, and deployment tools are simulated for reproducible action grading.

The data is built hierarchically. The authors first define the persona, relationships, and tools, then draft multi-year narratives, quarterly plans, weekly events, and conversations. Each test is verified with an oracle-memory procedure: an agent given the relevant history must be able to complete the task, while the same agent without that history must fail. This addresses common defects in synthetic benchmarks, such as incorrect labels, mislabeled evidence, or unanswerable tasks. Every submission must report accuracy, total cost, and median latency.1b:T605,The paper proceeds in three stages. First, it uses controlled referent-disambiguation settings to show that process failure is real and separable from perception. A model can enumerate all candidate objects and their attributes, yet still commit to a single unsupported answer. Second, the authors propose SRT, a family of structured process-prior interventions applied at inference time. SRT has three steps: State extraction, which explicitly surfaces observable states relevant to the question; Relevance checking, which tests whether those states are tied to the current decision boundary; and Target commitment, which delays the final answer until the previous checks are completed. This is more constrained than generic chain-of-thought because it specifies how evidence should be filtered and when commitment is allowed. Third, the paper introduces VPAC-Bench, a benchmark built around nine real-image process families. Each image is annotated not only with an activity stage but also with the nearby stage transition boundary, such as setup→active or active→finished. That structure lets the authors ask when a process prior helps: the key finding is that effectiveness depends on alignment between the supplied prior and the image’s actual decision boundary. Generic process prompts are not reliably helpful, and a misaligned prior can be worse than the default baseline. The paper’s main mechanism-level claim is that explicit reasoning helps only when its structure matches the latent decision boundary of the visual task.1c:T501,The empirical study has three parts. S1 uses COCO and GQA-style referent disambiguation to show that better visual access alone does not reliably repair the error, whereas a process-structured intervention can reduce cases where the model has already perceived the right evidence but still answers incorrectly. S2 evaluates SRT on spatial relation verification with VSR and finds strong model sensitivity: the same process prior improves some VLMs and hurts others, suggesting that the intervention interacts with internal representations rather than acting as a universally beneficial prompt template. S3 is the most informative section. VPAC-Bench covers nine real-image process families with human-reviewed labels and stage-transition annotations. The comparison among default prompting, generic process priors, and oracle-aligned priors shows that boundary alignment is the main driver of gains. The representative number highlighted in the paper is 86.1% versus 29.4% on physical state transition when using oracle-aligned versus misaligned settings, indicating that much of the gap comes from boundary identification and evidence binding rather than missing perception. The remaining bottleneck is automatic identification of which decision boundary applies to a given image.1d:T6ef,The paper has three methodological layers.

First, it measures shared errors directly. Across multiple preference datasets, the authors collect decisions from both open-weight and frontier judges, compute pairwise error correlations, and summarize the amount of independent information using effective sample size. They use the design-effect approximation: once average correlation is nonzero, a bank of n judges behaves like a much smaller number of independent judges in variance terms. The paper is careful to note that this does not mean 10 judges can literally be replaced by 3.5 judges; it means the consensus contains only that much independent statistical information.

Second, it studies how dependence changes conclusions. The paper compares a pooled-vote significance test, which treats each judge vote as an independent observation, against an item-level test that accounts for correlated votes on the same examples. This isolates a common evaluation mistake: inflating evidence by counting redundant votes as if they were independent samples.

Third, it distinguishes different structures of shared failure. The paper defines global co-failure, where many judges fail on the same items, and vulnerable-subgroup failure, where errors are concentrated within a subset of judges. The key point is that average correlation alone is not enough to distinguish these cases, yet they affect majority voting differently and call for different filtering rules. To address this, the authors propose CorrFilter, which discounts agreement among judges with correlated errors, and a bias-cluster filter, which ranks examples by support from judges outside the vulnerable subgroup. A small trusted set is then used to choose among majority voting and these specialized filters.1e:T731,The main quantitative result is straightforward and important. In the primary ten-judge bank, the average pairwise error correlation is 0.21. Under the design-effect approximation, this gives a variance inflation factor of about 2.85 and a variance-effective sample size of only n_eff ≈ 3.5. So ten judges do not provide ten units of independent evidence.

The paper repeats this analysis on two additional datasets and 73 alternative judge-bank compositions spanning prompt styles, model families, providers, and random seeds. The dependence is therefore not a one-off artifact of a single ensemble.

The frontier-judge result is even more striking. Three high-accuracy judges from different providers each reach 91% to 93% accuracy, yet their average error correlation is 0.56, and they make the same mistake together at 7.7 times the rate expected under independence. This directly challenges the naive intuition that stronger judges automatically make consensus more trustworthy. The paper also finds that judges from different providers are about as correlated as judges from the same provider, suggesting that shared blind spots are not just a same-family effect.

At the evaluation level, ignoring dependence changes significance conclusions in up to 28% of system comparisons: a pooled-vote test declares one system significantly better, while an item-level test that respects correlated votes does not. For filtering, the paper shows that no single fixed rule works best across both global co-failure and vulnerable-subgroup failure. A selector based on a small trusted set is tested on eight external factuality datasets, and in those transfers it always chooses plain majority voting. That result is useful because it positions the specialized filters as safeguards for specific failure modes rather than universal replacements.1f:T516,This is worth a careful skim for anyone building automatic evaluation, preference datasets, or RLHF/DPO filtering pipelines. Start with the introduction and the main figures to lock in the two headline numbers: rho = 0.21 implies n_eff ≈ 3.5 in the main ten-judge bank, and frontier judges reach rho = 0.56 despite high accuracy. Then read the methodology around effective sample size and pooled-vote versus item-level testing, because the paper is precise about what is and is not being measured. After that, focus on the shared-error-pattern analysis, especially the distinction between global co-failure and vulnerable-subgroup failure; that is the most conceptually useful part of the paper. Finish with the filtering experiments and the trusted-set selector to understand why the authors do not present CorrFilter as a universal default.

If time is limited, a good order is: Introduction, main result figures/tables, significance-testing section, failure-pattern section, then filtering. Around 30 minutes should be enough to extract the main lessons. Two practical takeaways stand out: report judge dependence or effective sample size rather than just the number of judges, and design retention rules for judge-labeled data at the item level instead of relying mechanically on raw vote counts.20:T893,方法上，这篇工作几乎没有把重点放在“发明一个更强的残差化器”上，而是建立了一套证据框架。作者固定了 measurement setup：需要一个原始分数、若干 surface features，以及一个与这些表面特征来源分离的 construct measurement。然后用同一个 residualization operator，在三种证据情境下分别检验它能支持什么主张。

第一种是 designed intervention。代码审计场景里，作者利用 unit-test correctness 作为外部构念标签，并只通过 comments/docstrings 改写来操纵格式通道，保证语义和正确性不变。这样就能直接测“格式效应是否被 attenuate”。这里 residualization 的角色不是生成新 gold score，而是作为审计探针：如果调整后，镜像格式条件下的偏好差距缩小，同时 correctness margin 基本不变，那么可以说它削弱了该格式通道。

第二种是 observational evidence。这里没有保标签干预，只能根据 surface-only predictor 的误差来定义某个 slice，再看 residualized score 在这个 slice 上是否与外部标注更一致。作者强调，这种设计只能支持 slice-restricted alignment gain，不能外推到总体有效性。原因很直接：slice 本身就是按某个模型的错误模式定义的，调整后在 slice 内变好，不代表总体更好，也不代表构念更纯。

第三种是 construct–artifact entanglement。作者构造并分析了构念与表面特征相关的情形，说明 decorrelation、loading 提升、slice gain 都不足以识别 validity restoration。换句话说，如果“好答案通常更长、更有注释、更像某种风格”本身就是任务分布的一部分，那么把这部分减掉，可能连有用信号一起减掉。

全文还有一个重要点：作者把 residualization 明确定位为 audit perturbation，而不是 repaired score。这一点和很多长度控制 win rate、judge debiasing 实践不同。作者还给出一套 reporting protocol，并允许 explicit refusal：证据不够时，就不声称分数被修复。这种写法比常见的“做了控制变量所以更公平”要严格得多。21:T7d0,The paper is organized around an evidence framework rather than a new estimator. The setup fixes three ingredients: an original score, a set of surface features, and a provenance-separated construct measurement. The same residualization operator is then examined under three evidential regimes.

First, in the designed-intervention regime, the authors use a code audit where unit-test execution provides correctness labels and only comments/docstrings manipulate the formatting channel. Because the intervention preserves correctness while changing format, attenuation of the format effect becomes directly measurable. Here residualization is used as an audit perturbation: if mirrored formatting conditions move closer after adjustment while correctness margins remain stable, one can claim attenuation of that specific surface channel.

Second, in the observational regime, no such intervention exists. The authors define slices using the errors of a surface-only predictor and test whether the residualized score aligns better with external annotations on that slice. The key point is that this supports only a slice-restricted alignment claim. Since the slice is itself induced by a model of surface effects, improvement inside the slice does not identify broader validity or better population-level measurement.

Third, the paper studies construct–artifact entanglement. When the target construct is genuinely correlated with the artifact, subtracting the predictable surface component can remove construct-relevant signal together with nuisance variation. In that case, lower correlation with artifacts, better loading diagnostics, or even slice gains do not identify validity restoration.

A central conceptual move is to treat residualization as an audit tool rather than a repaired score. That framing is stricter than common practice in length-controlled win rates or judge debiasing, and it leads to a reporting protocol with an explicit refusal option when the evidence is insufficient.22:T6a5,The most concrete experiment is the code preference audit. On MBPP-style pairs containing a terse correct solution and a commented buggy solution, a public preference reward model selects the correct answer only 0.507 of the time, essentially chance. When the verbosity–correctness pairing is reversed, the same model selects the correct answer 0.615 of the time, showing a substantial format effect. Under the designed intervention, residualization attenuates the format effect by about 0.12 on both correct and buggy code, shrinking the mirrored-preference gap from 0.108 to 0.037 while leaving correctness margins largely unchanged. Length-only corrections fail to close that gap, indicating that the issue is not reducible to raw length alone.

The paper also stresses the limits of this positive result. Across three held-out edit templates, the same operator overshoots one template, attenuates another, and amplifies a third. So even in a controlled setting, the effect of residualization is template- and channel-dependent.

In the observational analyses, held-out and disjoint-annotator tests support only a slice-level alignment gain. Every metric outside the declared slice declines after adjustment, including full-population alignment, the observable flagged subgroup, and within-question ranking. This is one of the strongest empirical points in the paper.

The construct–artifact entanglement section shows an even sharper failure mode: measured loading can improve while the adjusted judge becomes less useful. The paper reports four current-generation judge × setting probes, and all of them fall below the committed loading gate, meaning none supports a stronger validity claim.23:T985,作者先把 training-free UE 系统化分成三类，再在统一实验框架下比较。

第一类是 verbalized uncertainty。模型直接用自然语言表达不确定性，文中研究了两种形式：一种是 abstention，让模型在不确定时输出 UNKNOWN；另一种是 numerical confidence，在答案旁边给出置信分数。优点是部署简单、对闭源模型也适用、几乎没有额外推理成本；缺点是完全依赖模型的自我校准能力。

第二类是 token-level uncertainty。作者采用自回归输出分布上的 entropy 和 negative log-likelihood，并对整段回答做 mean 与 max 聚合。mean 代表长度归一化的整体不确定性，max 试图抓住局部薄弱 token 的尖峰。文中一个重要点是：在 deterministic decoding 下，这类信号会明显退化；提高采样温度后，校准后的效果能恢复，甚至接近更昂贵的 semantic 方法。这个观察很关键，因为它说明 token-level UE 不是简单读取 logits 就够了，解码策略本身决定了你能不能看到有区分度的分布信息。

第三类是 semantic uncertainty。作者纳入 Semantic Entropy 及其多模态变体 VL-Uncertainty、VASE 一类方法。核心思想是多次采样得到多个回答，再通过双向 entailment 或扰动一致性把表面不同但语义相同的回答聚类，在语义簇上计算熵。这样可以避免把“同义改写”误判成高不确定性。代价也很明显：需要多次推理、额外 entailment 模型和聚类开销。

实验框架上，作者沿四个轴系统比较：模型家族、模型代际、模型规模、回答长度。模型覆盖 InternVL 与 QwenVL 两个家族，且各自包含两代和两个规模档，小模型约 2B，中等模型约 7B 到 8B。任务覆盖五个 VQA benchmark，包含多选、句子级和开放式回答。评估指标除了常见 AUROC、Coverage at Risk，还加入 CHDA。这个 calibrated hallucination detection accuracy 指标会先在一小部分数据上校准阈值，再看部署式检测效果，比纯离线排序指标更接近实际使用场景。

方法层面的贡献不在于发明新 UE 算法，而在于把不同范式放到同一坐标系里，并明确指出每类方法的适用边界。尤其是“输出长度决定哪一类方法更占优”这一点，解释了为什么过去很多结论彼此矛盾：它们常常只在短答案 VQA 上成立。24:Ta31,The paper organizes training-free UE into three families and evaluates them under one experimental protocol.

The first family is verbalized uncertainty. The model expresses uncertainty in natural language. The authors study two variants: abstention, where the model outputs UNKNOWN when uncertain, and numerical confidence, where it emits a confidence score alongside the answer. These methods are easy to deploy, work for both open and closed models, and add almost no inference cost. Their weakness is obvious: they rely entirely on the model’s self-calibration.

The second family is token-level uncertainty. The paper uses entropy and negative log-likelihood from the autoregressive output distribution, aggregated over the response by mean and max. Mean is intended as a length-normalized global uncertainty score, while max captures localized spikes from weakly grounded tokens. A key empirical point is that these signals degrade badly under deterministic decoding, but recover when estimated at higher temperature. That is an important observation because it shows that token-level UE is not just about reading logits; the decoding regime determines whether the uncertainty signal remains informative.

The third family is semantic uncertainty. The study includes Semantic Entropy and multimodal variants such as VL-Uncertainty and VASE. The idea is to sample multiple responses, cluster them by semantic equivalence using bidirectional entailment or perturbation-based consistency, and compute entropy over semantic classes rather than surface forms. This avoids treating paraphrases as uncertainty. The trade-off is higher cost: multiple generations, an entailment model, and clustering overhead.

The evaluation protocol is one of the paper’s main strengths. The comparison spans four axes: model family, generation, size, and response-length regime. The models come from two MLLM families, InternVL and QwenVL, each with two generations and two scales, roughly 2B and 7-8B. The tasks cover five VQA benchmarks spanning multiple-choice, sentence-length, and open-ended outputs. In addition to AUROC and Coverage at Risk, the paper uses CHDA, a calibrated hallucination detection accuracy metric that calibrates a threshold on a small subset before deployment-style evaluation. That makes the analysis more operational than standard ranking-only metrics.

The contribution is therefore not a new UE algorithm. It is a careful empirical map of where each UE family works, where it breaks, and why earlier conclusions from short-answer benchmarks do not transfer cleanly to broader MLLM settings.25:T5fc,From the provided text, the confirmed setup includes two MLLM families, InternVL and QwenVL; two generations per family; two scale regimes, roughly 2B and 7-8B; and five VQA benchmarks covering multiple-choice, sentence-length, and open-ended response formats. The compared methods span verbalized, token-level, and semantic UE, evaluated with AUROC, Coverage at Risk, and CHDA.

The paper highlights four main findings. First, output length determines which UE family performs best; there is no single winner across all regimes. Second, token-level uncertainty collapses under deterministic decoding, but higher-temperature estimation restores calibrated signals that can rival much more expensive semantic methods. Third, verbalized self-uncertainty is unreliable in smaller and older MLLMs, but becomes much stronger in newer medium-scale models, with Qwen3-VL-Instruct called out explicitly. Fourth, even the better methods fail to achieve low risk at useful coverage, indicating that safe deployment of 2B to 8B MLLMs remains far from solved.

The excerpt does not include the full numeric tables, so it would be unsafe to quote exact margins. Still, the practical implications are already clear. For short-answer tasks, expensive semantic methods are not automatically justified. For long or open-ended outputs, plain token entropy is often unstable. For newer instruction-tuned models, abstention and verbalized confidence are worth testing seriously, but they should not be assumed to transfer backward to older generations.26:T44e,This is a solid skim-to-medium-depth read. Start with the taxonomy and the experimental setup. You need to understand what interface each UE family requires, what extra inference cost it incurs, and why token-level methods report both mean and max aggregation.

Then go to the main result figures, but do not look for a single average winner. The informative part is how rankings change when sliced by response length, model generation, and model size. That is where the paper’s strongest conclusions live.

Next, read the CHDA definition and threshold-calibration procedure. Many UE papers stop at AUROC, which is too far from deployment. This paper is more useful because it evaluates uncertainty in a calibrated decision setting.

Finally, read the discussion or failure-analysis sections, especially the explanation of why deterministic decoding destroys token-level signals and why verbalized uncertainty differs so much between older and newer models.

A reasonable reading budget is 30 to 45 minutes. If time is limited, read the method overview, the main tables/figures, and the CHDA section.27:T772,The method, VGCompiler, is built around two compilers. The representation compiler maps a visual graph into a structure-preserving intermediate graph representation. Its job is to recover nodes, edges, directions, weights, and relevant graph state as faithfully as possible. The operation compiler then decides which graph procedure should be executed for the given query under that recovered state. This matters because the correct executable procedure depends on the graph condition: reachability, cycle detection, and shortest path are different tasks, and shortest path itself depends on whether the graph is unweighted, nonnegative-weighted, or contains negative edges.

Instead of implementing these compilers as fixed symbolic modules, the paper uses a VLM to generate executable code. Code is treated as the primary reasoning object. During training, the model generates candidate code conditioned on a graph-question pair, and reinforcement learning is used to optimize generation. The reward is layered rather than answer-only: executability of the code, validity of the compiled graph, quality of the recovered representation, and quality of the chosen operation all contribute. This is an important design choice because it separates “correct answer for the wrong reason” from “mostly correct compilation but imperfect final execution.”

A second notable component is the code archive. A frozen observer extracts lightweight cues from each graph-question pair and uses them as condition signatures. Generated code is archived under these signatures during training, and compatible archived code can be retrieved at inference time. This is similar in spirit to case-based retrieval, but the retrieved object is executable program structure rather than natural-language reasoning traces. That makes sense for graph problems, where the space of valid operations is structured and reusable.28:T52c,The main instantiated model is Qwen-VGCompiler, obtained by RL fine-tuning Qwen3-VL-8B under the proposed framework. Evaluation covers three public benchmarks: GVLQA, VisionGraph, and VGCURE. The headline result reported in the paper is a 28.9% improvement over prior methods. From the full setup described in the introduction, the claim is framed as a broad gain across public benchmarks rather than a narrow win on one dataset.

A few experimental choices are worth attention. First, the paper goes beyond benchmark evaluation and tests three real-world scenarios: metro routing, logistics delivery, and network fault assessment. That matters because the central claim is about robustness of the compilation pipeline under heterogeneous visual graph styles, not just benchmark fitting. Second, the paper repeatedly emphasizes style, layout, and rendering variation as a source of failure, so the most informative ablations are likely those isolating the representation compiler, operation compiler, layered reward, and archive retrieval, especially under style shifts. Third, if the full tables break down results by graph state or by shortest-path variants, those sections are especially useful because they reveal whether the operation compiler truly learned state-aware execution rather than superficial task templates.29:T429,This is best read as a task decomposition paper, not as just another VLM reasoning method. Start with the introduction and focus on why the authors argue that the bottleneck is missing intermediate graph recovery rather than weak final-answer generation. Then read the method section for the exact definitions of the two compilers and the layered reward; if the reward terms are formalized clearly, it is worth checking how each term is made computable. After that, inspect the main result tables to see which baselines are used and where the reported 28.9% gain is concentrated. Finish with the real-world case studies and ablations to judge how much the code archive actually contributes.

If time is limited, a good order is: overview figures, method diagram, main benchmark table, then ablations. Around 20 to 30 minutes should be enough for a solid skim. The main deeper question is whether executable code can serve as a robust intermediate representation for other visually structured tasks such as tables, flowcharts, UI screenshots, or scientific diagrams.2a:T4a4,GAE uses a two-stage pipeline. In stage one, the DA3 geometry encoder is frozen, and a learned bottleneck is inserted between its four-level feature hierarchy and the original geometry head. The bottleneck compresses all feature levels into one latent and reconstructs the full hierarchy in a single pass. The geometry head remains frozen when decoding depth, camera rays, and point maps, which prevents the decoder from adapting around information lost during compression. An RGB head is trained from the same bottleneck, forcing appearance and geometry to share the latent. The latent is further shaped for generation through two constraints: token-wise alignment to co-located C-RADIO features improves semantic organization and smooth transport, while pairwise similarity matching to DINOv2 features restores relational and spatial structure. In stage two, the codec is frozen and a DiT-style conditional flow is trained in the standardized GAE latent space. The model uses text, metric Plücker rays, and reference views as conditions, with condition dropout enabling text-to-image generation, camera-controlled video, and reference-conditioned novel-view synthesis in one generator.2b:T4f9,WorldCrafter builds on latent video diffusion. A video VAE maps frames into spatiotemporal latents, and a DiT denoiser is trained with conditional flow matching. Long videos are generated chunk by chunk: recent clean latents are kept as a sliding temporal context, while the newly generated chunk is appended to the history archive. The new component is an implicit memory module. A memory encoder maps historical latent frames into a compact representation, initialized from pretrained 3D representation encoders to inherit a 3D inductive bias. The memory encoder, the pose-conditioned readout module, and the video DiT are then jointly optimized so the memory space co-adapts to the DiT token space. The key design is pose-guided readout: given the target camera trajectory, the model extracts a fixed number of memory tokens that are relevant to the requested viewpoints. These tokens are concatenated with recent-history tokens and current noisy latent tokens as [M; z_r; z_t] and are consumed directly by DiT self-attention. No explicit depth correspondence or target-view reconstruction is required. At inference time, complementary historical views are selected according to camera coverage, and few-step distillation reduces sampling cost for streaming interaction.2c:T426,The method represents a video as B latent chunks. Each chunk follows K noise levels and is denoised from x_b^K to x_b^0. A denoising step consists of a network prediction F_theta followed by a sampler update S_j. The key abstraction is a context-selection policy Phi_b(j,r), which chooses the noise state of historical chunk b-r when current chunk b is at denoising step j. This policy is tied to the history window W, attention masks, cached representations, sampler behavior, execution scheduling, and training-state sources. Three representative policies are studied: clean history uses clean endpoints from past chunks; same-level history uses past chunks at the same denoising level as the current chunk; progressive history makes more distant history cleaner. Compared with Self Forcing, which mainly addresses the source mismatch between training and inference histories, this work focuses on the denoising state of the historical context. Compared with pure streaming execution optimizations, it analyzes the modeling choice and execution graph together.2d:T734,The system, DuetGen, combines a Qwen3.5-2B autoregressive planner with a 4B single-stream DiT through what the authors call DeepFusion. Given a prompt, the planner emits a bbox-content plan, where each region contains four coordinates and a text string. Instead of feeding only the final serialized plan to the renderer, the method extracts the planner’s final-layer hidden states for both prompt tokens and plan tokens, then maps them through a lightweight projector into condition tokens for the DiT. This is the key design choice: the renderer conditions on structured continuous states, not just discrete layout symbols. Training uses three coordinated objectives. First, the planner is trained with the standard autoregressive next-token loss over the serialized plan. Second, the DiT is trained with a text-region-weighted flow-matching loss. Spatial locations inside rasterized reference text boxes receive a larger weight gamma, so the denoising objective focuses more strongly on text-bearing regions, where fidelity is hardest and most important. Third, the model adds auxiliary coordinate supervision: hidden states corresponding to each coordinate span are mean-pooled and regressed to the normalized bounding box. This head is used only during training, with the purpose of making geometric information more explicit in the planner states that later condition the DiT. At inference time, the model further applies Phase-Aware Attention Modulation, or PAM. PAM selectively strengthens attention from image patches inside a planned region to the matched bbox and content states, improving region-specific execution when multiple text regions or long strings compete for attention. The contribution is therefore not a simple pipeline combination, but a three-level coupling across representation, objective, and attention execution.2e:T481,From the provided full-content excerpt, the setup uses a 2B planner and a 4B DiT, with the DiT first pretrained from random initialization for general image generation before joint planning-rendering training. The experimental emphasis is on whether joint optimization improves multi-region and long-text generation, and whether the gains hold across planner scales and diffusion backbones. The paper studies the effect of text-region weighting, coordinate supervision, and PAM. The reported qualitative conclusion is that an explicit layout alone is not enough: without joint training, the renderer does not execute planner information as faithfully. Region-weighted diffusion improves text fidelity in local areas, coordinate supervision improves the spatial precision of planner representations, and PAM is especially useful when many text boxes or long strings are present. The abstract states that the method achieves competitive performance on multi-region and long-text generation. Since the available full text is truncated before the full result tables, it is not safe to invent exact benchmark numbers beyond the model sizes already specified.2f:T4a1,This is a skim paper, but the method section is worth reading if you care about unified generative modeling. Start with the introduction, especially the comparison among three paradigms: externally supplied layout plus diffusion, separated AR-diffusion systems, and fully autoregressive systems with discrete visual tokens. That framing explains why the authors choose neither a loose two-stage pipeline nor a pure AR image model. Then read the method carefully: the bbox-content serialization, the hidden-state mapping into DiT conditions, the text-region-weighted flow-matching loss, the auxiliary coordinate head, and PAM. The main idea is distributed across these components rather than concentrated in a single trick. In the experiments, prioritize the ablations that compare joint versus separated training and the analysis of PAM in multi-region settings. Expected reading time is about 20 to 30 minutes. Two useful follow-up questions are whether this hidden-state fusion pattern can transfer to speech or audio generation, and whether joint planner-renderer training introduces optimization conflicts that would benefit from a more explicit curriculum or alternating schedule.30:T665,Methodologically, this is a controlled reproduction plus comparison study. The authors adapt the planting probe from Wallat et al. to a setting built around Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct and three RLVR agents derived from it.

The procedure has three stages. First, the model answers a question given five retrieved passages, with exactly one citation per sentence. Second, a short span from the original claim is copied into a passage that was retrieved but not cited for that claim, and the model is run again on the doctored context. Third, the evaluation checks whether the citation for the same claim shifts to the doctored passage.

Several design choices matter. Since Qwen2.5 does not expose exact grounding spans, the planted span is selected deterministically as the four consecutive words in the claim with the most characters. Because the regenerated answer may be paraphrased, the authors do not simply ask whether the doctored citation appears anywhere. Instead, they align the new answer to the original claim using Jaccard overlap and score a claim-aligned outcome. The most important addition is the no-planting control. Rather than reporting raw planted rates, the paper asks how much citation movement is actually caused by the plant relative to a baseline where nothing is planted. This avoids conflating genuine post-rationalization with ordinary answer variability or self-repetition.

For the RLVR comparison, the setup is intentionally clean: one shared base model, fixed retrieval corpus, fixed prompt, and fixed scorer. That makes differences easier to attribute to training rather than to model family or infrastructure.31:T4f9,The experiments span four QA datasets. Three findings stand out.

First, post-rationalization is common even without RL. On Wikipedia-style datasets, roughly one citation in seven is unfaithful, around the 14% range. So the baseline instruction-tuned model already treats citation partly as a surface-matching task rather than strict evidence attribution.

Second, RLVR does not fix the problem. Under fixed-context inference, Search-R1 and ReSearch stay within a couple of percentage points of the base model, and R-Search is slightly worse. The authors also provide a detection bound: with their sample sizes, they would likely have detected an improvement of about seven percentage points, so a large hidden gain is unlikely.

Third, the control experiment matters. Even with no planting at all, the selected four-word span reappears in 60%–74% of regenerated answers, simply because models often reuse their own wording. This means evaluation protocols that condition on span recovery or report only raw planted rates can overstate the effect.

A useful detail is the distinction between claim-aligned scoring and cited-anywhere scoring. The latter is kept only as a sensitivity check and is shown to be looser. That strengthens the paper’s measurement credibility.32:T5ce,The interaction pattern is locate-correct-continue. An annotator reads a model response and marks the first inappropriate token. onPanda shows the model's top-k candidate tokens and probabilities at that position, with k defaulting to 20. If a suitable replacement is available, the annotator selects it; otherwise, the annotator writes a free-form replacement. The system truncates all later text and continues generation from the corrected prefix. The process repeats until the response is acceptable.

This differs from standard post-editing because the annotator does not repair a completed response after the model has already drifted. The continuation is re-rolled from the corrected prefix, so most final tokens remain model-generated. Each correction also creates a local supervision record: the bad token, the replacement, the prefix, and the model's candidate distribution. These records can be converted into SFT data, DPO pairs, reward-model data, or process-level supervision. Implementation-wise, onPanda is a componentized front-end library. It can run as a static web app or be embedded in an existing annotation platform. It requires an inference API that can continue from an assistant-message prefix and return token logprobs. A full annotation session is serialized into a single .panda.json file. For agents, it can access tools through MCP and connect to harnesses such as Claude Code, Codex, and OpenClaw, allowing corrected tool calls to be executed in the loop.33:T4a7,ACLArena builds a fixed sequential pipeline rather than evaluating arbitrary task permutations. The order is Math → Search → E-commerce → Instruction Following. Math strengthens reasoning, Search uses a single tool, E-commerce involves up to 15 tools, and Instruction Following is introduced last for adherence and alignment. Sequential training is used as the diagnostic baseline. The analysis has two levels: a model-level view that tracks capability changes across stages, and a token-level view that studies how individual training signals shape behavior and optimization. The method comparison covers three practical strategies. On-policy distillation uses previous checkpoints as teachers on the student’s current state distribution. Self-distilled fine-tuning replays filtered specialist trajectories offline. Model merging reuses prior models directly in parameter space. The proposed Mixture of Low-Rank Experts first uses SDFT to learn valid behaviors across domains, then applies RL through stage-specific LoRA adapters. A router composes the resulting experts. The design reduces interference by avoiding a single dense parameter update path for all heterogeneous skills.34:T402,The study uses emotion as the controlled concept because it is shared across text, audio, and image and can be represented with linear directions. The experiments cover Qwen2.5-Omni-7B, Phi-4-Multimodal-Instruct, and MiniCPM-o-4.5. For each layer, hidden states are captured with forward hooks and mean-pooled over tokens. Linear probes produce a layer-wise readability curve. Separately, emotion steering directions are injected at individual layers to produce a layer-wise steerability curve. The key claim is a probing–steering layer dissociation: probe-best layers are scattered across depth, while steer-best layers concentrate in a mid-to-late band. The paper uses random-direction controls, cross-modal settings, cross-model validation, and logit-lens analysis. The proposed explanation is that effective steering needs both readable representation and downstream plasticity; later layers may be readable but already too close to vocabulary commitment, while earlier layers may not yet provide a stable causal handle.35:T423,The method uses hRoPE, a hierarchical rotary positional encoding. Position is represented as three coordinates: p1 for paragraph index, p2 for sentence index inside a paragraph, and p3 for token index inside a sentence. Each level receives its own independent rotary channel group, so paragraph, sentence, and token displacements are not collapsed into a single scalar. The frequency base for each axis is calibrated from corpus statistics using the 99th-percentile coordinate range plus a safety margin, so low-frequency channels remain non-wrapping over typical ranges. The central experimental move is a counterfactual intervention: keep the tokens and lower-level coordinates fixed, modify only the paragraph coordinate p1, and measure cross-paragraph attention. A density-matched random-label channel with the same architecture is used as a control. This is important because an arbitrary extra positional axis can also compress attention; the diagnostic quantity is therefore not compression itself, but compression depth relative to the random control.36:T4a0,The paper combines three analyses. First, it elicits chain-of-thought explanations for evaluation/deployment classification from Gemma 3 1B, 4B, and 12B, Phi-3 Mini and Medium, and Llama-3 8B. The data includes 200 balanced prompts from SAD-Stages Oversight and a GSM8K-versus-WildChat split with 100 evaluation and 100 deployment prompts. Llama-3.3-70B is then used as a judge to assign the generated reasoning traces to categories such as task formatting, memorization, and meta-reasoning. Second, Integrated Gradients is applied to the Phi-3 family to identify input tokens that promote or inhibit evaluation awareness. Third, activation steering vectors are built from 204 contrastive prompts by taking the mean activation difference between evaluation and deployment contexts. A useful implementation detail is the extraction site: the authors use the token corresponding to the closing parenthesis of the model’s choice bracket, rather than static positions that may capture padding or EOS states. The proposed intervention has two paths: prompt sanitization removes external triggers, while activation counter-steering suppresses the internal evaluation-aware representation.37:Ta3d,方法核心是 TRACE：Teacher-forced Registry of Ablated Component Effects。做法不是像 activation patching 那样在 corrupted/clean 两条前向之间恢复激活，也不是训练 SAE 去解释残差特征，而是建立一个可注册的组件库，对每个组件单独做一次消融，然后在固定答案位置上看输出分布如何变化。

从全文描述看，这个框架有几个关键设计。第一，统一注册接口。它允许把不同层级的对象都视为“组件”，包括整个 transformer block、MHSA 子层、FFN 子层、单个神经元，甚至输出 logit 相关通道。这样做的意义是把不同粒度的解释对象放到同一实验协议下，而不是每种组件各用一套方法。

第二，teacher forcing。作者刻意把研究对象限制在“单个命名实体生成”，并在答案 token 位置固定上下文，避免自由生成时后续 token 误差传播污染 attribution。这样测到的是某个组件对目标 token 条件概率的直接支持，而不是对整段生成轨迹的混合影响。代价是它更适合 factual recall 场景，不适合 reasoning trace 很长的模型。

第三，行为数据集设计。作者构造了两个域：医疗药物知识和一般知识。医疗部分进一步限定到 drug 子域，选了 8 个药物实体，每个实体写 6 类线索提示，如历史、化学式、通用名、一线治疗、药物相互作用等，共 48 个 prompt。一般知识部分来自 TREC-8 QA，经改写后保证答案是单一命名实体，并筛选成与医疗域格式一致的问答。这个设计不是为了 benchmark 分数，而是为了区分“跨域都重要的通用生成组件”和“只在某一域显著的专门组件”。

第四，消融效应度量。摘要里明确说是测量 ablation 后固定答案 token 输出分布的变化。虽然截取内容里没有完整公式，但可以确定它不是只看 top-1 是否翻转，而是看目标答案概率或相关分布量的下降幅度，因此能得到连续的贡献分数。相比只做正确/错误二值统计，这种度量更细，也更适合比较不同组件类型。

与已有方法相比，它的区别很清楚。相对 activation patching，它不依赖构造 clean/corrupted 对照样本，也不强调“恢复足够性”，而是强调“去掉后损失多少支持”。相对 SAE，它不需要额外训练解释模型，扩展到不同模型家族更直接。相对 circuit discovery，它牺牲了组合级最小电路发现能力，换来在当代 dense LLM 上可扩展的单组件扫描。这个取舍很现实。38:Ta5f,The core method is TRACE, short for Teacher-forced Registry of Ablated Component Effects. Instead of activation patching between clean and corrupted runs, and instead of training a sparse autoencoder over residual activations, the framework registers model components at multiple granularities, ablates them one by one, and measures the effect on the output distribution at a fixed answer position.

Several design choices matter. First, the registry abstraction. Different objects can be treated uniformly as components: full transformer blocks, MHSA sublayers, FFN sublayers, individual neurons, and even output-logit-related units. This is the main reason the framework can compare heterogeneous component types under one protocol.

Second, teacher forcing is central. The study is intentionally restricted to prompts whose answer is a single named entity, and the answer position is evaluated with the surrounding context fixed. This avoids contamination from autoregressive error propagation and makes the score closer to direct support for the target token rather than support for an entire generated trajectory. The trade-off is obvious: the setup is well suited to factual recall, not to long reasoning traces.

Third, the behavioral dataset is purpose-built. The authors use two domains: medical knowledge and general knowledge. The medical side is narrowed to drugs, with 8 entities and 6 clue categories per entity, for 48 prompts total. The general-knowledge side is adapted from TREC-8 QA and rewritten so that each answer is a single named entity. This is not benchmark construction for accuracy; it is an experimental design for separating domain-general generation components from domain-specific ones.

Fourth, the effect metric is distributional rather than purely binary. From the abstract and method description, the framework measures how the output distribution at the fixed answer token changes after ablation. Even without the full formula in the provided excerpt, it is clear that the method is not limited to whether top-1 flips. That makes the signal more continuous and more suitable for comparing components across prompts.

Relative to prior work, the positioning is clear. Compared with activation patching, TRACE does not require clean/corrupted paired inputs and focuses on loss of support after removal rather than sufficiency after restoration. Compared with SAE-based analyses, it avoids training an auxiliary model. Compared with circuit discovery, it gives up combinatorial minimal-circuit identification in exchange for tractable large-scale scans on contemporary dense LMs. That is a sensible engineering trade-off.39:T54b,From the provided full-text excerpt, the confirmed setup is as follows: the study targets dense language models; the task is single-entity QA; the dataset includes 48 medical prompts plus a filtered and rewritten set of TREC-8 general-knowledge prompts. The authors describe the study as a large-scale ablation experiment, but the excerpt does not include the final result tables or reliable quantitative numbers, so it would be unsafe to invent layer-wise or component-wise effect sizes.

Still, the experimental intent is clear. The paper compares multiple component granularities to test whether entity knowledge is localized or distributed. It explicitly separates domain-general from domain-specific supporting components, so the main results should include contribution distributions aggregated by domain rather than only global averages. The multiple clue categories per drug entity are also a good design choice: they help distinguish support for the entity itself from support for parsing a particular clue style.

In practical terms, the paper should be used for directional conclusions unless one has access to the full result figures and tables. What can be stated confidently is that the authors built a scalable single-component ablation framework and applied it systematically to named-entity recall in both specialized and general domains.3a:T403,This is a skim paper. Focus on three parts.

First, read the method section carefully enough to understand the TRACE score. The key question is whether the metric is based on target-token probability drop, logit difference, or some normalized contribution measure. That determines whether comparisons across prompts and component types are meaningful.

Second, read the dataset and prompting design. The restrictions to single named entities and teacher forcing are not incidental; they define the scope of the claims. The paper is about factual entity recall, not general reasoning.

Third, inspect the main result figures for component rankings across granularities and for the split between common and domain-specific components. If FFN, attention, and block-level effects differ substantially by domain, that is the part most relevant to model editing and continual pretraining.

A practical reading budget is 20 to 30 minutes. If time is tight, read the introduction, the method overview, and the main result figures only.3b:T668,The method has two layers: a diagnostic argument and a generative model. First, the authors show that the observed intransitivities cannot be explained by a single latent utility under any monotone link function, not merely under the standard Bradley–Terry parameterization. This matters because it rules out a broad family of single-ordering noise models. To avoid confounding latent plurality with prompt artifacts, the data collection protocol controls for prompt wording, presentation order, and option-label bias using multiple prompt variants, both item orders, randomized pseudo-identifiers, and repeated sampling.

The proposed model is a noise-augmented mixture Bradley–Terry (MBT). Each comparison is generated by first sampling a latent preference component, then drawing a pairwise choice according to that component’s Bradley–Terry probabilities, with an additional noise term to absorb residual randomness. Relative to a standard BT model, the key change is not just extra flexibility. Each component remains transitive and interpretable as a coherent ordering, while the observed aggregate can be intransitive because it mixes across components. The paper also uses posterior effective component count, computed from the perplexity of mixture weights, to estimate how many components are actually active for a given model-task pair.

Conceptually, this imports mixture-based preference modeling from social choice and preference learning into LLM behavior analysis. It differs from standard reward-model aggregation by allowing one model to sample from multiple coherent preference modes rather than one global scale.3c:T6ca,The study evaluates 7 open-weight LLMs on 4 pairwise-comparison tasks: two factual domains—US city populations as an easier numerical task and atomic half-lives as a harder scientific task—and two subjective domains—joke preferences and Moral Machine dilemmas. This yields 29 model-dataset combinations. The collection protocol is deliberately heavy: every unordered item pair is queried under 4 prompt variants, both presentation orders, and repeated samples, so the authors can estimate stable pairwise choice distributions rather than one-off answers.

There are four main findings. First, structured intransitivity is real. The nonparametric diagnostic rejects the idea that a single latent ordering with noise explains the data, and this remains true after controlling for prompt, order, and label effects. Second, MBT improves held-out preference prediction over a single-component Bradley–Terry baseline across models and tasks, indicating that the mixture structure is not merely overfitting training noise. Third, the posterior effective number of components is often greater than one, meaning multiple active preference modes are needed in many model-task settings. Fourth, aggregate rankings can be misleading for model comparison: two models that look aligned in aggregate may disagree at the component level, and models that look different overall may share similar latent components.

An important nuance is that mixture structure appears in both factual and subjective tasks. That makes the phenomenon harder to dismiss as only a moral-values issue. Harder factual tasks may expose switching between heuristics under uncertainty, while subjective tasks may reflect switching between normative or aesthetic criteria.3d:T46d,For a quick read, focus on three parts. First, read the introduction and background carefully to understand exactly what is being rejected: not just vanilla Bradley–Terry, but the broader single-ordering-plus-noise view. Second, read the MBT formulation and the role of the explicit noise augmentation. The key idea is how aggregate intransitivity can emerge while each latent component remains transitive. Third, inspect the results on held-out prediction, effective component count, and aggregate-versus-disaggregated model comparisons. Those sections carry most of the paper’s practical message.

If time is limited, prioritize the introduction, the model definition, Figure 1, Table 2, and the sections showing how aggregate comparisons hide component-level differences. Around 30 to 45 minutes should be enough to extract the main argument. The most interesting follow-up questions are whether the inferred components correlate with controllable factors such as system prompts, temperature, or framing, and whether the same mixture view can improve reward-model training rather than serving only as an offline analysis tool.3e:T69a,The method combines paired task synthesis with two-stage post-training. The authors distinguish two safety settings. In environment-risk tasks, the user goal is benign but the interface contains hazards; the correct behavior is safe continuation, not refusal. In explicit-misuse tasks, the goal itself is harmful and should be refused. This distinction is central, because collapsing both into a single refuse/not-refuse label would push the model toward conservative shutdown behavior.

To support this, they introduce SCOPE-Gen, an automated pipeline that starts from normal GUI tasks and injects detectable forbidden events or risky interface elements while preserving the original user goal and completion evaluator. This produces paired tasks and three complementary trajectory types: capability trajectories for ordinary execution, risk-handling trajectories for safe continuation under environmental hazards, and refusal trajectories for harmful goals or no-safe-path cases. The reward is defined as the product of completion reward and safety reward, so full credit requires both successful completion and hazard avoidance.

Training proceeds in two stages. First, joint SFT teaches the model all three behaviors together, establishing a conditional execution policy. Second, capability-oriented online RL further improves task completion while monitoring whether safety is retained. The design choice is deliberate: use supervised data to shape the decision boundary first, then use RL to push capability, rather than expecting RL to discover the right safety-capability tradeoff from scratch. The instantiated model is based on Qwen3.5-9B, and the trajectory dataset is SATraj-OS.3f:T510,The main evaluations are on OSWorld for capability and OS-BLIND for attack avoidance. The headline numbers are clear. Capability-only training improves execution but does not reliably induce attack avoidance. After joint capability-safety SFT, SCOPE-SFT reaches 49.72% on OSWorld and 66.30% attack-avoidance on OS-BLIND, with a capability-safety harmonic mean of 56.83%. After subsequent capability-oriented online RL, SCOPE-RL improves OSWorld to 54.17% while retaining 64.30% attack avoidance, yielding the best aggregate score of 58.80%.

The ablations are the most informative part. Refusal trajectories account for most of the gain in attack avoidance on OS-BLIND, meaning that explicit supervision is effective for teaching when to stop. Risk-handling trajectories play a different role: they preserve task utility at comparable attack-avoidance levels by teaching the model to continue safely instead of over-refusing. Together, the two trajectory types produce a more balanced conditional policy.

The broader takeaway from the training path—capability-only, then joint SFT, then capability-oriented RL—is that safety does not emerge automatically from capability optimization, but once a safety-aware boundary is learned, further capability improvement does not necessarily erase it.40:T594,VideoGen-Agent uses a shared multimodal policy in a reasoning-action-observation loop. At each step, the policy conditions on the prompt, previous actions, and tool observations, then selects a tool and specifies its arguments. The six task categories are Procedural Knowledge, Single-Entity Identity, Multi-Entity Identity, Physics Simulation, Compositional Scene, and Multi-Shot. Each category has a default tool subset and workflow: procedural prompts use text retrieval for steps and motion details; identity prompts use visual retrieval and image-conditioned or multi-reference generation; physics prompts invoke simulators and pass motion conditions to a motion-conditioned generator; compositional prompts generate candidates and use verification tools such as detection and depth estimation for regeneration; multi-shot prompts generate phases sequentially and use the last frame of each segment to preserve continuity. Training has two stages. First, the policy is supervised on teacher-distilled tool-use trajectories, with the paper mentioning Claude Opus 4.7 as part of the trajectory construction process. Second, the policy is optimized with multitask agentic RL. A hybrid reward scores both tool use and generated video quality, while task advantage normalization balances learning signals across the six categories. Unicode sequences at illegal boundaries, producing standalone combining marks such as dependent vowels or viramas and causing unreadable model outputs.","importance":"The useful lesson for pretraining is that tokenizer quality is not only about compression or frequency statistics. For abugida scripts such as Devanagari, Telugu, Tamil, Kannada, and Gujarati, token boundaries must respect orthographic constraints. Otherwise the model is trained on atomic units that are not valid written units. This affects multilingual pretraining data quality, token budgets, and language modeling performance for under-served scripts. The contribution is a formal and implementable tokenizer constraint, rather than a new LM objective.","technical_approach":"The authors frame the issue algebraically. Alphabetic scripts such as English can be treated roughly as a semigroup: concatenating two valid fragments usually gives another valid fragment. Brahmic scripts behave like a partial semigroup: concatenation is only valid when the boundary satisfies script-specific transition constraints. The paper formalizes this distinction in Agda, encodes Brahmic orthography as a typed transition system, and defines valid tokens as chains in that system. A fixToken function is then derived with machine-checked proofs for validity preservation, remainder correctness, and completeness. The formal construction is translated into a SentencePiece patch and a configurable Rust pre-tokenizer.","experiments":"The empirical section studies malformed tokenization in Telugu, Tamil, Hindi, and Gujarati. Perplexity drops from 166 to 85 for Unigram and from 159 to 92 for BPE. appr","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-26T04:07:52Z","category_id":"pretraining_data","cover_url":"/covers/2609_22145.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文处理 diffusion LLM 的训练数据检测问题：在没有自回归模型那种单次前向即可分解序列似然的条件下，如何用有限查询预算得到稳定的成员检测信号。核心难点不是简单的采样不足，而是被同时 mask 的 token 之间存在依赖，随机采样后把 token-wise 概率相乘会引入系统性估计偏差。","importance":"对预训练研究的启发主要在 diffusion / masked LM 的隐私审计和概率估计接口。AR 模型的训练样本检测通常依赖序列 log-likelihood 或 token-level loss；dLLM 的概率由不同 mask 配置下的重构分布给出，检测信号会受 mask 组合影响。论文把这个问题明确成“用可查询的 token-wise 概率近似 joint probability 时的结构误差”，并指出误差与 masked token
